Other paths, even combinations of remaining edges, will be severed due to the intrinsic blocking nature of severed connections.

--- Model Response ---
Step-by-step reasoning:

The target nodes are **H** and **L**. I will analyze all possible paths between these nodes and determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to disconnect them.

1. **Identify all paths from H to L**:
   - H → G → F → M → L
   - H → G → X → F → M → L
   - H → G → X → K → J → I → C → N → B → L
   - H → G → X → K → J → I → P → C → N → B → L
   - H → G → X → K → J → I → P → O
   - H → B → N → C → I → J → K → X → F → M → L
   - H → B → N → C → I → P → O
   - H → B → L (direct edge)
   - H → B → N → O

2. **Analyze critical edges**:
   - Removing any single edge from these paths won't completely disconnect H from L since there are overlapping edges and alternative paths.
   - To ensure complete disconnection, multiple critical edges need to be removed.

3. **Identify the minimal cut set**:
   - The critical edges that need to be removed are:
     - (H, B) : This breaks the direct connection.
     - (B, N) : This disrupts multiple paths going through B.
     - (N, C) : This stops paths from B towards the lower network.
     - If (M, L) were part of this cut set, it would be removed, but since it would make the cut set unexplored, (B, L) is removed instead.

Removing these edges ensures that there are no alternative paths remaining between **H** and **L**.

Final Answer:
Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(H, B), (B, N), (B, L)]

- **Path 3:** H -> G -> F -> X -> K -> J -> I -> C -> N
- **Path 4:** H -> G -> F -> X -> K -> J -> I -> P -> D -> O -> N
- **Path 5:** H -> G -> F -> X -> K -> J -> P -> D -> O -> N

- **Path 1** can be broken by removing B or H (H cannot be removed).
- **Path 2** can be broken by removing any node in the sequence H -> G -> F -> M -> L -> E -> B -> N.
- **Path 3** can be broken by removing any node in the sequence H -> G -> F -> X -> K -> J -> I -> C -> N.

- Removing J will break Paths 3, 4, and 5.

- **Edge I-J** needs to be removed because nodes I and J are central connectors to D and must be disconnected entirely.

     - N → Y → P → I → H

   - Removing the edge (P, J) will break the path: N → Y → P → J → X → H.
